## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me analyze the issue description:

1. There's a spider file named `y.py` with its compiled bytecode `y.pyc` in the spider directory
2. The user renames the file from `y.py` to `x.py` using `mv y.py x.py`
3. They update the content of `x.py`
4. When running `scrapy crawl spider`, it uses the old version from `y.pyc` instead of the updated `x.py`
5. The workaround is to manually remove `y.pyc`

Key technical details:
- File names: `y.py`, `y.pyc`, `x.py`
- Command: `mv y.py x.py`
- Command: `scrapy crawl spider`
- Directory: spider directory

Problem explanation:
This is a classic Python bytecode caching issue. When Python compiles a `.py` file, it creates a corresponding `.pyc` file in either the same directory or in a `__pycache__` directory. When a file is renamed but the old `.pyc` file remains, Python's import mechanism might still load the old bytecode file instead of recompiling the new file.

Steps to reproduce:
1. Have a spider file `y.py` with corresponding `y.pyc`
2. Rename `y.py` to `x.py` 
3. Update content of `x.py`
4. Run `scrapy crawl spider`
5. Observe that old version from `y.pyc` is used instead of new `x.py`

Best practices for fixing:
- Need to handle stale bytecode files properly during spider loading
- Should automatically detect and clean up orphaned .pyc files
- Should ensure proper module reloading/recompilation
- Minimal changes to avoid breaking existing functionality</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 1.3 Problem Explanation
The issue occurs when a Scrapy spider file is renamed. Python creates `.pyc` (compiled bytecode) files alongside `.py` files. When a spider file is renamed (e.g., from `y.py` to `x.py`), the old `.pyc` file (`y.pyc`) remains in the directory. Scrapy's spider loader incorrectly loads the old bytecode file instead of the newly renamed and updated spider file.

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Create a spider file `y.py` with corresponding `y.pyc` in the spiders directory
2. Rename the file from `y.py` to `x.py` using `mv y.py x.py`
3. Update the content of `x.py`
4. Run `scrapy crawl spider`
5. Observe that the old version from `y.pyc` is used instead of the updated `x.py`

### 1.5 Best Practices
- Handle stale bytecode files properly during spider loading
- Automatically detect and manage orphaned `.pyc` files
- Ensure proper module reloading/recompilation
- Make minimal changes to avoid breaking existing functionality

    30	crawl websites and extract structured data from their pages. It can be used for
    31	a wide range of purposes, from data mining to monitoring and automated testing.

     1	# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
     2	from __future__ import absolute_import
     3	import traceback
     4	import warnings
     5	
     6	from zope.interface import implementer
     7	
     8	from scrapy.interfaces import ISpiderLoader
     9	from scrapy.utils.misc import walk_modules
    10	from scrapy.utils.spider import iter_spider_classes
    11	
    12	
    13	@implementer(ISpiderLoader)
    14	class SpiderLoader(object):
    15	    """
    16	    SpiderLoader is a class which locates and loads spiders
    17	    in a Scrapy project.
    18	    """
    19	    def __init__(self, settings):
    20	        self.spider_modules = settings.getlist('SPIDER_MODULES')
    21	        self._spiders = {}
    22	        self._load_all_spiders()
    23	
    24	    def _load_spiders(self, module):
    25	        for spcls in iter_spider_classes(module):
    26	            self._spiders[spcls.name] = spcls
    27	
    28	    def _load_all_spiders(self):
    29	        for name in self.spider_modules:
    30	            try:
    31	                for module in walk_modules(name):
    32	                    self._load_spiders(module)
    33	            except ImportError as e:
    34	                msg = ("\n{tb}Could not load spiders from module '{modname}'. "
    35	                       "Check SPIDER_MODULES setting".format(
    36	                            modname=name, tb=traceback.format_exc()))
    37	                warnings.warn(msg, RuntimeWarning)
    38	
    39	    @classmethod
    40	    def from_settings(cls, settings):
    41	        return cls(settings)
    42	
    43	    def load(self, spider_name):
    44	        """
    45	        Return the Spider class for the given spider name. If the spider
    46	        name is not found, raise a KeyError.
    47	        """
    48	        try:
    49	            return self._spiders[spider_name]
    50	        except KeyError:
    51	            raise KeyError("Spider not found: {}".format(spider_name))
    52	
    53	    def find_by_request(self, request):
    54	        """
    55	        Return the list of spider names that can handle the given request.
    56	        """
    57	        return [name for name, cls in self._spiders.items()
    58	                if cls.handles_request(request)]
    59	
    60	    def list(self):
    61	        """
    62	        Return a list with the names of all spiders available in the project.
    63	        """
    64	        return list(self._spiders.keys())
    65

    14	_ITERABLE_SINGLE_VALUES = dict, BaseItem, six.text_type, bytes
    15	
    16	
    17	def arg_to_iter(arg):
    18	    """Convert an argument to an iterable. The argument can be a None, single
    19	    value, or an iterable.
    20	
    21	    Exception: if arg is a dict, [arg] will be returned
    22	    """
    23	    if arg is None:
    24	        return []
    25	    elif not isinstance(arg, _ITERABLE_SINGLE_VALUES) and hasattr(arg, '__iter__'):
    26	        return arg
    27	    else:
    28	        return [arg]
    29	
    30	
    31	def load_object(path):
    32	    """Load an object given its absolute object path, and return it.
    33	
    34	    object can be a class, function, variable or an instance.
    35	    path ie: 'scrapy.downloadermiddlewares.redirect.RedirectMiddleware'
    36	    """
    37	
    38	    try:
    39	        dot = path.rindex('.')
    40	    except ValueError:
    41	        raise ValueError("Error loading object '%s': not a full path" % path)
    42	
    43	    module, name = path[:dot], path[dot+1:]
    44	    mod = import_module(module)
    45	
    46	    try:
    47	        obj = getattr(mod, name)
    48	    except AttributeError:
    49	        raise NameError("Module '%s' doesn't define any object named '%s'" % (module, name))
    50	
    51	    return obj
    52	
    53	
    54	def walk_modules(path):
    55	    """Loads a module and all its submodules from the given module path and
    56	    returns them. If *any* module throws an exception while importing, that
    57	    exception is thrown back.
    58	
    59	    For example: walk_modules('scrapy.utils')
    60	    """
    61	
    62	    mods = []
    63	    mod = import_module(path)
    64	    mods.append(mod)
    65	    if hasattr(mod, '__path__'):
    66	        for _, subpath, ispkg in iter_modules(mod.__path__):
    67	            fullpath = path + '.' + subpath
    68	            if ispkg:
    69	                mods += walk_modules(fullpath)
    70	            else:
    71	                submod = import_module(fullpath)
    72	                mods.append(submod)
    73	    return mods
    74	
    75	
    76	def extract_regex(regex, text, encoding='utf-8'):
    77	    """Extract a list of unicode strings from the given text/encoding using the following policies:
    78	
    79	    * if the regex contains a named group called "extract" that will be returned
    80	    * if the regex contains multiple numbered groups, all those will be returned (flattened)
    81	    * if the regex doesn't contain any group the entire regex matching is returned
    82	    """
    83	
    84	    if isinstance(regex, six.string_types):
    85	        regex = re.compile(regex, re.UNICODE)
    86	
    87	    try:
    88	        strings = [regex.search(text).group('extract')]   # named group
    89	    except:
    90	        strings = regex.findall(text)    # full regex or numbered groups
    91	    strings = flatten(strings)
    92	
    93	    if isinstance(text, six.text_type):
    94	        return [replace_entities(s, keep=['lt', 'amp']) for s in strings]
    95	    else:
    96	        return [replace_entities(to_unicode(s, encoding), keep=['lt', 'amp'])
    97	                for s in strings]
    98	
    99	
   100	def md5sum(file):
   101	    """Calculate the md5 checksum of a file-like object without reading its
   102	    whole content in memory.
   103	
   104	    >>> from io import BytesIO
   105	    >>> md5sum(BytesIO(b'file content to hash'))
   106	    '784406af91dd5a54fbb9c84c2236595a'
   107	    """
   108	    m = hashlib.md5()
   109	    while True:
   110	        d = file.read(8096)
   111	        if not d:
   112	            break
   113	        m.update(d)
   114	    return m.hexdigest()
   115	
   116	
   117	def rel_has_nofollow(rel):
   118	    """Return True if link rel attribute has nofollow type"""
   119	    return True if rel is not None and 'nofollow' in rel.split() else False
   120

    13	    return arg_to_iter(result)

    16	def iter_spider_classes(module):
    17	    """Return an iterator over all spider classes defined in the given module
    18	    that can be instantiated (ie. which have name)
    19	    """
    20	    # this needs to be imported here until get rid of the spider manager
    21	    # singleton in scrapy.spider.spiders
    22	    from scrapy.spiders import Spider
    23	
    24	    for obj in six.itervalues(vars(module)):
    25	        if inspect.isclass(obj) and \
    26	           issubclass(obj, Spider) and \
    27	           obj.__module__ == module.__name__ and \
    28	           getattr(obj, 'name', None):
    29	            yield obj
    30	
    31	def spidercls_for_request(spider_loader, request, default_spidercls=None,
    32	                          log_none=False, log_multiple=False):
    33	    """Return a spider class that handles the given Request.
    34	
    35	    This will look for the spiders that can handle the given request (using
    36	    the spider loader) and return a Spider class if (and only if) there is
    37	    only one Spider able to handle the Request.
    38	
    39	    If multiple spiders (or no spider) are found, it will return the
    40	    default_spidercls passed. It can optionally log if multiple or no spiders
    41	    are found.
    42	    """
    43	    snames = spider_loader.find_by_request(request)
    44	    if len(snames) == 1:
    45	        return spider_loader.load(snames[0])
    46	
    47	    if len(snames) > 1 and log_multiple:
    48	        logger.error('More than one spider can handle: %(request)s - %(snames)s',
    49	                     {'request': request, 'snames': ', '.join(snames)})
    50	
    51	    if len(snames) == 0 and log_none:
    52	        logger.error('Unable to find spider that handles: %(request)s',
    53	                     {'request': request})
    54	
    55	    return default_spidercls
    56	
    57	
    58	class DefaultSpider(Spider):
    59	    name = 'default'

     8	class Command(ScrapyCommand):
     9	
    10	    requires_project = True
    11	
    12	    def syntax(self):
    13	        return "[options] <spider>"
    14	
    15	    def short_desc(self):
    16	        return "Run a spider"
    17	
    18	    def add_options(self, parser):
    19	        ScrapyCommand.add_options(self, parser)
    20	        parser.add_option("-a", dest="spargs", action="append", default=[], metavar="NAME=VALUE",
    21	                          help="set spider argument (may be repeated)")
    22	        parser.add_option("-o", "--output", metavar="FILE",
    23	                          help="dump scraped items into FILE (use - for stdout)")
    24	        parser.add_option("-t", "--output-format", metavar="FORMAT",
    25	                          help="format to use for dumping items with -o")
    26	
    27	    def process_options(self, args, opts):
    28	        ScrapyCommand.process_options(self, args, opts)
    29	        try:
    30	            opts.spargs = arglist_to_dict(opts.spargs)
    31	        except ValueError:
    32	            raise UsageError("Invalid -a value, use -a NAME=VALUE", print_help=False)
    33	        if opts.output:
    34	            if opts.output == '-':
    35	                self.settings.set('FEED_URI', 'stdout:', priority='cmdline')
    36	            else:
    37	                self.settings.set('FEED_URI', opts.output, priority='cmdline')
    38	            feed_exporters = without_none_values(
    39	                self.settings.getwithbase('FEED_EXPORTERS'))
    40	            valid_output_formats = feed_exporters.keys()
    41	            if not opts.output_format:
    42	                opts.output_format = os.path.splitext(opts.output)[1].replace(".", "")
    43	            if opts.output_format not in valid_output_formats:
    44	                raise UsageError("Unrecognized output format '%s', set one"
    45	                                 " using the '-t' switch or as a file extension"
    46	                                 " from the supported list %s" % (opts.output_format,
    47	                                                                  tuple(valid_output_formats)))
    48	            self.settings.set('FEED_FORMAT', opts.output_format, priority='cmdline')
    49	
    50	    def run(self, args, opts):
    51	        if len(args) < 1:
    52	            raise UsageError()
    53	        elif len(args) > 1:
    54	            raise UsageError("running 'scrapy crawl' with more than one spider is no longer supported")
    55	        spname = args[0]
    56	
    57	        self.crawler_process.crawl(spname, **opts.spargs)
    58	        self.crawler_process.start()

    25	class Crawler(object):
    26	
    27	    def __init__(self, spidercls, settings=None):
    28	        if isinstance(settings, dict) or settings is None:
    29	            settings = Settings(settings)
    30	
    31	        self.spidercls = spidercls
    32	        self.settings = settings.copy()
    33	        self.spidercls.update_settings(self.settings)
    34	
    35	        self.signals = SignalManager(self)
    36	        self.stats = load_object(self.settings['STATS_CLASS'])(self)
    37	
    38	        handler = LogCounterHandler(self, level=settings.get('LOG_LEVEL'))
    39	        logging.root.addHandler(handler)
    40	        # lambda is assigned to Crawler attribute because this way it is not
    41	        # garbage collected after leaving __init__ scope
    42	        self.__remove_handler = lambda: logging.root.removeHandler(handler)
    43	        self.signals.connect(self.__remove_handler, signals.engine_stopped)
    44	
    45	        lf_cls = load_object(self.settings['LOG_FORMATTER'])
    46	        self.logformatter = lf_cls.from_crawler(self)
    47	        self.extensions = ExtensionManager.from_crawler(self)
    48	
    49	        self.settings.freeze()
    50	        self.crawling = False
    51	        self.spider = None
    52	        self.engine = None
    53	
    54	    @property
    55	    def spiders(self):
    56	        if not hasattr(self, '_spiders'):
    57	            warnings.warn("Crawler.spiders is deprecated, use "
    58	                          "CrawlerRunner.spider_loader or instantiate "
    59	                          "scrapy.spiderloader.SpiderLoader with your "
    60	                          "settings.",
    61	                          category=ScrapyDeprecationWarning, stacklevel=2)
    62	            self._spiders = _get_spider_loader(self.settings.frozencopy())
    63	        return self._spiders
    64	
    65	    @defer.inlineCallbacks
    66	    def crawl(self, *args, **kwargs):
    67	        assert not self.crawling, "Crawling already taking place"
    68	        self.crawling = True
    69	
    70	        try:
    71	            self.spider = self._create_spider(*args, **kwargs)
    72	            self.engine = self._create_engine()
    73	            start_requests = iter(self.spider.start_requests())
    74	            yield self.engine.open_spider(self.spider, start_requests)
    75	            yield defer.maybeDeferred(self.engine.start)
    76	        except Exception:
    77	            # In Python 2 reraising an exception after yield discards
    78	            # the original traceback (see http://bugs.python.org/issue7563),
    79	            # so sys.exc_info() workaround is used.
    80	            # This workaround also works in Python 3, but it is not needed,
    81	            # and it is slower, so in Python 3 we use native `raise`.
    82	            if six.PY2:
    83	                exc_info = sys.exc_info()
    84	
    85	            self.crawling = False
    86	            if self.engine is not None:
    87	                yield self.engine.close()
    88	
    89	            if six.PY2:
    90	                six.reraise(*exc_info)
    91	            raise
    92	
    93	    def _create_spider(self, *args, **kwargs):
    94	        return self.spidercls.from_crawler(self, *args, **kwargs)
    95	
    96	    def _create_engine(self):
    97	        return ExecutionEngine(self, lambda _: self.stop())
    98	
    99	    @defer.inlineCallbacks
   100	    def stop(self):
   101	        if self.crawling:
   102	            self.crawling = False
   103	            yield defer.maybeDeferred(self.engine.stop)
   104	
   105	
   106	class CrawlerRunner(object):
   107	    """
   108	    This is a convenient helper class that keeps track of, manages and runs
   109	    crawlers inside an already setup Twisted `reactor`_.
   110	
   111	    The CrawlerRunner object must be instantiated with a
   112	    :class:`~scrapy.settings.Settings` object.
   113	
   114	    This class shouldn't be needed (since Scrapy is responsible of using it
   115	    accordingly) unless writing scripts that manually handle the crawling
   116	    process. See :ref:`run-from-script` for an example.
   117	    """
   118	
   119	    crawlers = property(
   120	        lambda self: self._crawlers,
   121	        doc="Set of :class:`crawlers <scrapy.crawler.Crawler>` started by "
   122	            ":meth:`crawl` and managed by this class."
   123	    )
   124	
   125	    def __init__(self, settings=None):
   126	        if isinstance(settings, dict) or settings is None:
   127	            settings = Settings(settings)
   128	        self.settings = settings
   129	        self.spider_loader = _get_spider_loader(settings)
   130	        self._crawlers = set()
   131	        self._active = set()
   132	
   133	    @property
   134	    def spiders(self):
   135	        warnings.warn("CrawlerRunner.spiders attribute is renamed to "
   136	                      "CrawlerRunner.spider_loader.",
   137	                      category=ScrapyDeprecationWarning, stacklevel=2)
   138	        return self.spider_loader
   139	
   140	    def crawl(self, crawler_or_spidercls, *args, **kwargs):
   141	        """
   142	        Run a crawler with the provided arguments.
   143	
   144	        It will call the given Crawler's :meth:`~Crawler.crawl` method, while
   145	        keeping track of it so it can be stopped later.
   146	
   147	        If `crawler_or_spidercls` isn't a :class:`~scrapy.crawler.Crawler`
   148	        instance, this method will try to create one using this parameter as
   149	        the spider class given to it.
   150	
   151	        Returns a deferred that is fired when the crawling is finished.
   152	
   153	        :param crawler_or_spidercls: already created crawler, or a spider class
   154	            or spider's name inside the project to create it
   155	        :type crawler_or_spidercls: :class:`~scrapy.crawler.Crawler` instance,
   156	            :class:`~scrapy.spiders.Spider` subclass or string
   157	
   158	        :param list args: arguments to initialize the spider
   159	
   160	        :param dict kwargs: keyword arguments to initialize the spider
   161	        """
   162	        crawler = self.create_crawler(crawler_or_spidercls)
   163	        return self._crawl(crawler, *args, **kwargs)
   164	
   165	    def _crawl(self, crawler, *args, **kwargs):
   166	        self.crawlers.add(crawler)
   167	        d = crawler.crawl(*args, **kwargs)
   168	        self._active.add(d)
   169	
   170	        def _done(result):
   171	            self.crawlers.discard(crawler)
   172	            self._active.discard(d)
   173	            return result
   174	
   175	        return d.addBoth(_done)
   176	
   177	    def create_crawler(self, crawler_or_spidercls):
   178	        """
   179	        Return a :class:`~scrapy.crawler.Crawler` object.
   180	
   181	        * If `crawler_or_spidercls` is a Crawler, it is returned as-is.
   182	        * If `crawler_or_spidercls` is a Spider subclass, a new Crawler
   183	          is constructed for it.
   184	        * If `crawler_or_spidercls` is a string, this function finds
   185	          a spider with this name in a Scrapy project (using spider loader),
   186	          then creates a Crawler instance for it.
   187	        """
   188	        if isinstance(crawler_or_spidercls, Crawler):
   189	            return crawler_or_spidercls
   190	        return self._create_crawler(crawler_or_spidercls)
   191	
   192	    def _create_crawler(self, spidercls):
   193	        if isinstance(spidercls, six.string_types):
   194	            spidercls = self.spider_loader.load(spidercls)
   195	        return Crawler(spidercls, self.settings)
   196	
   197	    def stop(self):
   198	        """
   199	        Stops simultaneously all the crawling jobs taking place.
   200	
   201	        Returns a deferred that is fired when they all have ended.
   202	        """
   203	        return defer.DeferredList([c.stop() for c in list(self.crawlers)])
   204	
   205	    @defer.inlineCallbacks
   206	    def join(self):
   207	        """
   208	        join()
   209	
   210	        Returns a deferred that is fired when all managed :attr:`crawlers` have
   211	        completed their executions.
   212	        """
   213	        while self._active:
   214	            yield defer.DeferredList(self._active)
   215	
   216	
   217	class CrawlerProcess(CrawlerRunner):
   218	    """
   219	    A class to run multiple scrapy crawlers in a process simultaneously.
   220	
   221	    This class extends :class:`~scrapy.crawler.CrawlerRunner` by adding support
   222	    for starting a Twisted `reactor`_ and handling shutdown signals, like the
   223	    keyboard interrupt command Ctrl-C. It also configures top-level logging.
   224	
   225	    This utility should be a better fit than
   226	    :class:`~scrapy.crawler.CrawlerRunner` if you aren't running another
   227	    Twisted `reactor`_ within your application.
   228	
   229	    The CrawlerProcess object must be instantiated with a
   230	    :class:`~scrapy.settings.Settings` object.
   231	
   232	    This class shouldn't be needed (since Scrapy is responsible of using it
   233	    accordingly) unless writing scripts that manually handle the crawling
   234	    process. See :ref:`run-from-script` for an example.
   235	    """
   236	
   237	    def __init__(self, settings=None):
   238	        super(CrawlerProcess, self).__init__(settings)
   239	        install_shutdown_handlers(self._signal_shutdown)
   240	        configure_logging(self.settings)
   241	        log_scrapy_info(self.settings)
   242	
   243	    def _signal_shutdown(self, signum, _):
   244	        install_shutdown_handlers(self._signal_kill)
   245	        signame = signal_names[signum]
   246	        logger.info("Received %(signame)s, shutting down gracefully. Send again to force ",
   247	                    {'signame': signame})
   248	        reactor.callFromThread(self._graceful_stop_reactor)
   249	
   250	    def _signal_kill(self, signum, _):
   251	        install_shutdown_handlers(signal.SIG_IGN)
   252	        signame = signal_names[signum]
   253	        logger.info('Received %(signame)s twice, forcing unclean shutdown',
   254	                    {'signame': signame})
   255	        reactor.callFromThread(self._stop_reactor)
   256	
   257	    def start(self, stop_after_crawl=True):
   258	        """
   259	        This method starts a Twisted `reactor`_, adjusts its pool size to
   260	        :setting:`REACTOR_THREADPOOL_MAXSIZE`, and installs a DNS cache based
   261	        on :setting:`DNSCACHE_ENABLED` and :setting:`DNSCACHE_SIZE`.
   262	
   263	        If `stop_after_crawl` is True, the reactor will be stopped after all
   264	        crawlers have finished, using :meth:`join`.
   265	
   266	        :param boolean stop_after_crawl: stop or not the reactor when all
   267	            crawlers have finished
   268	        """
   269	        if stop_after_crawl:
   270	            d = self.join()
   271	            # Don't start the reactor if the deferreds are already fired
   272	            if d.called:
   273	                return
   274	            d.addBoth(self._stop_reactor)
   275	
   276	        reactor.installResolver(self._get_dns_resolver())
   277	        tp = reactor.getThreadPool()
   278	        tp.adjustPoolsize(maxthreads=self.settings.getint('REACTOR_THREADPOOL_MAXSIZE'))
   279	        reactor.addSystemEventTrigger('before', 'shutdown', self.stop)
   280	        reactor.run(installSignalHandlers=False)  # blocking call
   281	
   282	    def _get_dns_resolver(self):
   283	        if self.settings.getbool('DNSCACHE_ENABLED'):
   284	            cache_size = self.settings.getint('DNSCACHE_SIZE')
   285	        else:
   286	            cache_size = 0
   287	        return CachingThreadedResolver(
   288	            reactor=reactor,
   289	            cache_size=cache_size,
   290	            timeout=self.settings.getfloat('DNS_TIMEOUT')
   291	        )
   292	
   293	    def _graceful_stop_reactor(self):
   294	        d = self.stop()
   295	        d.addBoth(self._stop_reactor)
   296	        return d
   297	
   298	    def _stop_reactor(self, _=None):
   299	        try:
   300	            reactor.stop()
   301	        except RuntimeError:  # raised if already stopped or in shutdown stage
   302	            pass
   303	
   304	
   305	def _get_spider_loader(settings):
   306	    """ Get SpiderLoader instance from settings """
   307	    if settings.get('SPIDER_MANAGER_CLASS'):
   308	        warnings.warn(
   309	            'SPIDER_MANAGER_CLASS option is deprecated. '
   310	            'Please use SPIDER_LOADER_CLASS.',
   311	            category=ScrapyDeprecationWarning, stacklevel=2
   312	        )
   313	    cls_path = settings.get('SPIDER_MANAGER_CLASS',
   314	                            settings.get('SPIDER_LOADER_CLASS'))
   315	    loader_cls = load_object(cls_path)
   316	    try:
   317	        verifyClass(ISpiderLoader, loader_cls)
   318	    except DoesNotImplement:
   319	        warnings.warn(
   320	            'SPIDER_LOADER_CLASS (previously named SPIDER_MANAGER_CLASS) does '
   321	            'not fully implement scrapy.interfaces.ISpiderLoader interface. '
   322	            'Please add all missing methods to avoid unexpected runtime errors.',
   323	            category=ScrapyDeprecationWarning, stacklevel=2
   324	        )
   325	    return loader_cls.from_settings(settings.frozencopy())
   326

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now let's understand the problem better. Let me check the Python import mechanism and how it relates to .pyc files:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the code, I can see the issue flow:

1. In `spiderloader.py`, `_load_all_spiders()` calls `walk_modules()` from `utils.misc`
2. `walk_modules()` uses `import_module()` to load modules
3. When a spider file is renamed (e.g., from `y.py` to `x.py`), the old `y.pyc` file remains
4. Python's import mechanism will still find and load the old `y.pyc` file even though `y.py` no longer exists
5. This causes the old spider code to be loaded instead of the new one

The root issue is in the `walk_modules` function in `scrapy/utils/misc.py`. When it walks through modules using `iter_modules` and `import_module`, Python's import system caches the modules and may load stale .pyc files.

Looking at the code, the issue is that:
1. `walk_modules` uses `iter_modules(mod.__path__)` to get module names
2. Then it calls `import_module(fullpath)` to load each module
3. Python's import system may load cached .pyc files even when the source .py file has been renamed

The solution would be to somehow invalidate or clean up stale .pyc files before loading modules, or to ensure that when walking modules, we don't load stale bytecode files.

Let me check if there are any existing mechanisms for dealing with this in Python or in Scrapy.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me investigate how Python handles .pyc files and what the issue might be:<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/scrapy__scrapy__1.3 && python -c "

     1	import sys, os
     2	
     3	from scrapy.commands import ScrapyCommand
     4	from scrapy.exceptions import UsageError
     5	
     6	class Command(ScrapyCommand):
     7	
     8	    requires_project = True
     9	    default_settings = {'LOG_ENABLED': False}
    10	
    11	    def syntax(self):
    12	        return "<spider>"
    13	
    14	    def short_desc(self):
    15	        return "Edit spider"
    16	
    17	    def long_desc(self):
    18	        return "Edit a spider using the editor defined in EDITOR setting"
    19	
    20	    def _err(self, msg):
    21	        sys.stderr.write(msg + os.linesep)
    22	        self.exitcode = 1
    23	
    24	    def run(self, args, opts):
    25	        if len(args) != 1:
    26	            raise UsageError()
    27	
    28	        editor = self.settings['EDITOR']
    29	        try:
    30	            spidercls = self.crawler_process.spider_loader.load(args[0])
    31	        except KeyError:
    32	            return self._err("Spider not found: %s" % args[0])
    33	
    34	        sfile = sys.modules[spidercls.__module__].__file__
    35	        sfile = sfile.replace('.pyc', '.py')

"""
Script to reproduce the .pyc file loading issue in Scrapy spider loader.
"""
import os
import sys
import tempfile
import shutil
import importlib
from pathlib import Path

# Add the scrapy directory to the path
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/scrapy__scrapy__1.3')

from scrapy.settings import Settings
from scrapy.spiderloader import SpiderLoader


def create_test_spider_file(directory, filename, spider_name, content):
    """Create a spider file with the given content."""
    filepath = os.path.join(directory, filename)
    with open(filepath, 'w') as f:
        f.write(content)
    return filepath


def test_pyc_issue():
    """Test the .pyc file loading issue."""
    # Create a temporary directory for our test
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='scrapy_pyc_test_')
    spiders_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, 'test_spiders')
    os.makedirs(spiders_dir)
    
    try:
        print(f"Created test directory: {test_dir}")
        
        # Add the test directory to Python path
        sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
        
        # Create initial spider file y.py
        spider_content_v1 = '''
import scrapy

class TestSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"
    start_urls = ["http://example.com"]
    
    def parse(self, response):
        return {"version": "v1"}
'''
        
        spider_file_y = create_test_spider_file(spiders_dir, 'y.py', 'test_spider', spider_content_v1)
        print(f"Created spider file: {spider_file_y}")
        
        # Import the module to generate .pyc file
        init_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__init__.py')
        with open(init_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write('')
            
        # Load the module to generate .pyc
        sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
        settings = Settings({'SPIDER_MODULES': ['test_spiders']})
        spider_loader = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Check if spider can be loaded
        try:
            spider_cls = spider_loader.load('test_spider')
            print(f"Loaded spider: {spider_cls.__name__}")
            print(f"Spider module: {spider_cls.__module__}")
            print(f"Spider file: {sys.modules[spider_cls.__module__].__file__}")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error loading spider: {e}")
            
        # Now rename the file from y.py to x.py  
        spider_file_x = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'x.py')
        os.rename(spider_file_y, spider_file_x)
        print(f"Renamed spider file to: {spider_file_x}")
        
        # Update the content of the new file
        spider_content_v2 = '''
import scrapy

class TestSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"
    start_urls = ["http://example.com"]
    
    def parse(self, response):
        return {"version": "v2"}  # Updated version
'''
        
        with open(spider_file_x, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content_v2)
        print("Updated spider content to v2")
        
        # Reload spider loader to simulate fresh start
        # Remove from sys.modules to force reload
        modules_to_remove = [k for k in sys.modules.keys() if k.startswith('test_spiders')]
        for mod in modules_to_remove:
            del sys.modules[mod]
            
        # Create new spider loader
        spider_loader_new = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Try to load the spider
        try:
            spider_cls_new = spider_loader_new.load('test_spider')
            print(f"Loaded spider after rename: {spider_cls_new.__name__}")
            print(f"Spider module: {spider_cls_new.__module__}")
            spider_module = sys.modules[spider_cls_new.__module__]
            print(f"Spider file: {spider_module.__file__}")
            
            # Check if it's the updated version
            # We can check by looking at the source or by checking if .pyc file exists
            source_file = spider_module.__file__
            if 'v2' in source_file:
                print("SUCCESS: Loaded updated spider")
            else:
                print("ISSUE: May have loaded old spider")
                
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error loading spider after rename: {e}")
            
        # Check for .pyc files
        pyc_files = list(Path(spiders_dir).glob('*.pyc'))
        print(f"Found .pyc files: {pyc_files}")
        
        for pyc_file in Path(spiders_dir).rglob('__pycache__/*.pyc'):
            print(f"Found __pycache__ .pyc file: {pyc_file}")
            
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        if test_dir in sys.path:
            sys.path.remove(test_dir)
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)

"""
Script to reproduce the .pyc file loading issue in Scrapy spider loader.
"""
import os
import sys
import tempfile
import shutil
import importlib
from pathlib import Path

# Add the scrapy directory to the path
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/scrapy__scrapy__1.3')

from scrapy.settings import Settings
from scrapy.spiderloader import SpiderLoader


def create_test_spider_file(directory, filename, spider_name, content):
    """Create a spider file with the given content."""
    filepath = os.path.join(directory, filename)
    with open(filepath, 'w') as f:
        f.write(content)
    return filepath


def test_pyc_issue():
    """Test the .pyc file loading issue."""
    # Create a temporary directory for our test
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='scrapy_pyc_test_')
    spiders_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, 'test_spiders')
    os.makedirs(spiders_dir)
    
    try:
        print(f"Created test directory: {test_dir}")
        
        # Add the test directory to Python path
        sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
        
        # Create initial spider file y.py
        spider_content_v1 = '''
import scrapy

class TestSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"
    start_urls = ["http://example.com"]
    
    def parse(self, response):
        return {"version": "v1"}
'''
        
        spider_file_y = create_test_spider_file(spiders_dir, 'y.py', 'test_spider', spider_content_v1)
        print(f"Created spider file: {spider_file_y}")
        
        # Import the module to generate .pyc file
        init_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__init__.py')
        with open(init_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write('')
            
        # Load the module to generate .pyc
        sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
        settings = Settings({'SPIDER_MODULES': ['test_spiders']})
        spider_loader = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Check if spider can be loaded
        try:
            spider_cls = spider_loader.load('test_spider')
            print(f"Loaded spider: {spider_cls.__name__}")
            print(f"Spider module: {spider_cls.__module__}")
            print(f"Spider file: {sys.modules[spider_cls.__module__].__file__}")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error loading spider: {e}")
            
        # Force creation of .pyc file by importing directly
        import importlib.util
        spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("test_spiders.y", spider_file_y)
        module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
        spec.loader.exec_module(module)
        print("Direct import to generate .pyc file")
        
        # Now rename the file from y.py to x.py  
        spider_file_x = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'x.py')
        os.rename(spider_file_y, spider_file_x)
        print(f"Renamed spider file to: {spider_file_x}")
        
        # Update the content of the new file
        spider_content_v2 = '''
import scrapy

class TestSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"
    start_urls = ["http://example.com"]
    
    def parse(self, response):
        return {"version": "v2"}  # Updated version
'''
        
        with open(spider_file_x, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content_v2)
        print("Updated spider content to v2")
        
        # Reload spider loader to simulate fresh start
        # Remove from sys.modules to force reload
        modules_to_remove = [k for k in sys.modules.keys() if k.startswith('test_spiders')]
        for mod in modules_to_remove:
            del sys.modules[mod]
            
        # Create new spider loader
        spider_loader_new = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Try to load the spider
        try:
            spider_cls_new = spider_loader_new.load('test_spider')
            print(f"Loaded spider after rename: {spider_cls_new.__name__}")
            print(f"Spider module: {spider_cls_new.__module__}")
            spider_module = sys.modules[spider_cls_new.__module__]
            print(f"Spider file: {spider_module.__file__}")
            
            # Check if it's the updated version
            # Read the actual file content to check version
            with open(spider_module.__file__, 'r') as f:
                content = f.read()
                if 'v2' in content:
                    print("SUCCESS: Loaded updated spider (contains v2)")
                elif 'v1' in content:
                    print("ISSUE: Loaded old spider (contains v1)")
                else:
                    print("UNKNOWN: Could not determine spider version")
                
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error loading spider after rename: {e}")
            
        # Check for .pyc files
        pyc_files = list(Path(spiders_dir).glob('*.pyc'))
        print(f"Found .pyc files: {pyc_files}")
        
        for pyc_file in Path(spiders_dir).rglob('__pycache__/*.pyc'):
            print(f"Found __pycache__ .pyc file: {pyc_file}")
            
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        if test_dir in sys.path:
            sys.path.remove(test_dir)
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)


def test_direct_pyc_issue():
    """Test the direct .pyc file loading issue."""

"""
Script to reproduce the exact .pyc file loading issue in Scrapy spider loader.
This mimics the exact scenario: having y.py and y.pyc, then renaming to x.py
"""
import os
import sys
import tempfile
import shutil
import importlib
import importlib.util
import py_compile
from pathlib import Path

# Add the scrapy directory to the path
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/scrapy__scrapy__1.3')

from scrapy.settings import Settings
from scrapy.spiderloader import SpiderLoader


def create_test_spider_file(directory, filename, spider_name, content):
    """Create a spider file with the given content."""
    filepath = os.path.join(directory, filename)
    with open(filepath, 'w') as f:
        f.write(content)
    return filepath


def test_exact_pyc_issue():
    """Test the exact .pyc file loading issue from the bug report."""
    # Create a temporary directory for our test
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='scrapy_pyc_exact_test_')
    spiders_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, 'test_spiders')
    os.makedirs(spiders_dir)
    
    try:
        print(f"=== Exact .pyc Issue Test ===")
        print(f"Created test directory: {test_dir}")
        
        # Add the test directory to Python path
        sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
        
        # Create initial spider file y.py
        spider_content_v1 = '''import scrapy

class TestSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"
    start_urls = ["http://example.com"]
    
    def parse(self, response):
        return {"version": "v1_original"}
'''
        
        spider_file_y = create_test_spider_file(spiders_dir, 'y.py', 'test_spider', spider_content_v1)
        print(f"Created spider file: {spider_file_y}")
        
        # Create __init__.py
        init_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__init__.py')
        with open(init_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write('')
            
        # Manually compile to .pyc to simulate the scenario where both .py and .pyc exist
        pycache_dir = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__pycache__')
        os.makedirs(pycache_dir, exist_ok=True)
        
        # Compile y.py to y.pyc (in __pycache__ format for Python 3)
        y_pyc = os.path.join(pycache_dir, 'y.cpython-39.pyc')
        py_compile.compile(spider_file_y, y_pyc, doraise=True)
        print(f"Compiled y.py to: {y_pyc}")
        
        # Load once to register the spider
        settings = Settings({'SPIDER_MODULES': ['test_spiders']})
        spider_loader = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Check if spider can be loaded from y
        try:
            spider_cls = spider_loader.load('test_spider')
            print(f"Loaded spider from y.py: {spider_cls.__name__}")
            spider_module = sys.modules[spider_cls.__module__]
            print(f"Spider file: {spider_module.__file__}")
            
            # Read content to verify version
            with open(spider_module.__file__, 'r') as f:
                content = f.read()
                if 'v1_original' in content:
                    print("SUCCESS: Loaded original spider (contains v1_original)")
                else:
                    print("UNEXPECTED: Original spider content issue")
                    
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error loading spider from y: {e}")
        
        # NOW SIMULATE THE RENAME SCENARIO FROM THE BUG REPORT
        # mv y.py x.py
        spider_file_x = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'x.py')
        os.rename(spider_file_y, spider_file_x)
        print(f"Renamed y.py to x.py: {spider_file_x}")
        
        # Update x.py content (as mentioned in bug report: "update the new x.py")
        spider_content_v2 = '''import scrapy

class TestSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"
    start_urls = ["http://example.com"]
    
    def parse(self, response):
        return {"version": "v2_updated"}
'''
        
        with open(spider_file_x, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content_v2)
        print("Updated x.py content to v2_updated")
        
        # IMPORTANT: The y.pyc file still exists from before!
        print(f"y.pyc still exists: {os.path.exists(y_pyc)}")
        
        # Clear sys.modules to simulate fresh start (like running scrapy crawl)
        modules_to_remove = [k for k in sys.modules.keys() if k.startswith('test_spiders')]
        for mod in modules_to_remove:
            del sys.modules[mod]
            
        # Create NEW spider loader (simulating scrapy crawl command)
        print("\n--- Creating new spider loader (simulating scrapy crawl) ---")
        spider_loader_new = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Try to load the spider - this should demonstrate the issue
        try:
            spider_cls_new = spider_loader_new.load('test_spider')
            print(f"Loaded spider after rename: {spider_cls_new.__name__}")
            spider_module_new = sys.modules[spider_cls_new.__module__]
            print(f"Spider module: {spider_cls_new.__module__}")
            print(f"Spider file: {spider_module_new.__file__}")
            
            # Read the actual file content to check which version was loaded
            with open(spider_module_new.__file__, 'r') as f:
                content = f.read()
                print(f"Actual file content contains:")
                if 'v2_updated' in content:
                    print("  ✓ SUCCESS: Loaded updated spider (contains v2_updated)")
                elif 'v1_original' in content:
                    print("  ✗ ISSUE: Loaded OLD spider (contains v1_original)")
                else:
                    print("  ? UNKNOWN: Could not determine spider version")
                print(f"Content preview: {content[:100]}...")
                
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error loading spider after rename: {e}")
            import traceback
            traceback.print_exc()
            
        # Show all .pyc files
        print(f"\nExisting .pyc files:")
        for pyc_file in Path(spiders_dir).rglob('__pycache__/*.pyc'):
            print(f"  {pyc_file}")
            
        # The issue: y.pyc still exists and might interfere with loading
        if os.path.exists(y_pyc):
            print(f"\n🐛 BUG CONFIRMED: Old y.pyc file still exists and could cause issues!")
        else:
            print(f"\n✅ No old .pyc file found")
            
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        if test_dir in sys.path:
            sys.path.remove(test_dir)
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)

The problem is that when we rename `y.py` to `x.py`, the Python module system is looking for `test_spiders.x` but can't find it because the module discovery is based on the filesystem. The `walk_modules` function uses `iter_modules` which looks at the actual `.py` files, but when it tries to import `test_spiders.x`, it fails because the module name doesn't match.

However, this is slightly different from the exact issue described. """
Script to reproduce the real .pyc file loading issue.
The issue is that when you have y.pyc and rename y.py to x.py,

"""
import os
import sys
import tempfile
import shutil
import importlib
import py_compile
from pathlib import Path

# Add the scrapy directory to the path
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/scrapy__scrapy__1.3')

from scrapy.settings import Settings
from scrapy.spiderloader import SpiderLoader


def create_test_spider_file(directory, filename, spider_name, content):
    """Create a spider file with the given content."""
    filepath = os.path.join(directory, filename)
    with open(filepath, 'w') as f:
        f.write(content)
    return filepath


def test_real_scenario():
    """Test a realistic scenario where the issue occurs."""
    # Create a temporary directory for our test
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='scrapy_real_pyc_test_')
    spiders_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, 'test_spiders')
    os.makedirs(spiders_dir)
    
    try:
        print(f"=== Real Scenario Test ===")
        print(f"Created test directory: {test_dir}")
        
        # Add the test directory to Python path
        sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
        
        # Create spider with name matching the filename (common pattern)
        spider_content_y_v1 = '''import scrapy

class YSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "y_spider"
    start_urls = ["http://example.com"]
    
    def parse(self, response):
        return {"version": "y_v1_original"}
'''

        spider_file_y = create_test_spider_file(spiders_dir, 'y.py', 'y_spider', spider_content_y_v1)
        print(f"Created spider file: {spider_file_y}")
        
        # Create __init__.py
        init_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__init__.py')
        with open(init_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write('')
            
        # Load once to create .pyc files in __pycache__
        settings = Settings({'SPIDER_MODULES': ['test_spiders']})
        spider_loader_initial = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Check initial load
        try:
            spider_cls_initial = spider_loader_initial.load('y_spider')
            print(f"✓ Initial load successful: {spider_cls_initial.__name__}")
            spider_module_initial = sys.modules[spider_cls_initial.__module__]
            print(f"  Module: {spider_cls_initial.__module__}")
            print(f"  File: {spider_module_initial.__file__}")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"✗ Error on initial load: {e}")
        
        # Show .pyc files created
        print(f"\nAfter initial load, .pyc files:")
        pycache_dir = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__pycache__')
        if os.path.exists(pycache_dir):
            for pyc_file in Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc'):
                print(f"  {pyc_file}")
        
        # NOW SIMULATE THE REAL RENAME SCENARIO
        print(f"\n--- Simulating mv y.py x.py ---")
        
        # Actually rename the file
        spider_file_x = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'x.py')
        os.rename(spider_file_y, spider_file_x)
        print(f"Renamed to: {spider_file_x}")
        
        # Update content to indicate it's now x.py spider
        spider_content_x_v2 = '''import scrapy

class XSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "x_spider"  # Changed name to match new filename
    start_urls = ["http://example.com"]
    
    def parse(self, response):
        return {"version": "x_v2_updated"}
'''
        
        with open(spider_file_x, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content_x_v2)
        print("Updated content for x.py")
        
        # Clear sys.modules to simulate fresh start
        modules_to_remove = [k for k in sys.modules.keys() if k.startswith('test_spiders')]
        for mod in modules_to_remove:
            del sys.modules[mod]
            
        # Create NEW spider loader (simulating scrapy crawl command)
        print(f"\n--- Creating new spider loader ---")
        spider_loader_new = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # List available spiders
        available_spiders = spider_loader_new.list()
        print(f"Available spiders: {available_spiders}")
        
        # Try to load the new spider
        try:
            spider_cls_new = spider_loader_new.load('x_spider')
            print(f"✓ Successfully loaded x_spider: {spider_cls_new.__name__}")
            spider_module_new = sys.modules[spider_cls_new.__module__]
            print(f"  Module: {spider_cls_new.__module__}")
            print(f"  File: {spider_module_new.__file__}")
            
            # Check content
            with open(spider_module_new.__file__, 'r') as f:
                content = f.read()
                if 'x_v2_updated' in content:
                    print("  ✓ Correct content loaded (x_v2_updated)")
                else:
                    print("  ? Unexpected content")
                    
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"✗ Could not load x_spider: {e}")
            
        # Try to load old spider name (should fail)
        try:
            spider_loader_new.load('y_spider')
            print("✗ ERROR: y_spider should not be loadable!")
        except KeyError:
            print("✓ Correctly failed to load y_spider (no longer exists)")
            
        # Show final .pyc situation
        print(f"\nFinal .pyc files:")
        if os.path.exists(pycache_dir):
            for pyc_file in Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc'):
                print(f"  {pyc_file}")
                
        # The real issue comes when there are conflicting .pyc files
        # Let's simulate a case where both y.pyc and x.pyc exist but with wrong content
        
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        if test_dir in sys.path:
            sys.path.remove(test_dir)
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)


def test_stale_pyc_scenario():
    """Test the specific scenario with stale .pyc files causing wrong loads."""
    # Create a temporary directory for our test
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='scrapy_stale_pyc_test_')
    spiders_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, 'test_spiders')
    os.makedirs(spiders_dir)
    
    try:
        print(f"\n=== Stale .pyc Scenario Test ===")
        print(f"Created test directory: {test_dir}")
        
        # Add the test directory to Python path
        sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
        
        # Create initial spider file y.py
        spider_content_y_v1 = '''import scrapy

class TestSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"
    start_urls = ["http://example.com"]
    
    def parse(self, response):
        return {"source": "y_file", "version": "v1_original"}
'''

        spider_file_y = create_test_spider_file(spiders_dir, 'y.py', 'test_spider', spider_content_y_v1)
        print(f"Created spider file: {spider_file_y}")
        
        # Create __init__.py
        init_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__init__.py')
        with open(init_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write('')
            
        # Manually create y.pyc to simulate stale bytecode
        pycache_dir = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__pycache__')
        os.makedirs(pycache_dir, exist_ok=True)
        y_pyc = os.path.join(pycache_dir, 'y.cpython-39.pyc')
        py_compile.compile(spider_file_y, y_pyc, doraise=True)
        print(f"Created y.pyc: {y_pyc}")
        
        # Now rename y.py to x.py and update content
        spider_file_x = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'x.py')
        shutil.copy2(spider_file_y, spider_file_x)  # Copy first
        os.remove(spider_file_y)  # Then remove (simulate mv)
        print(f"Renamed y.py to x.py: {spider_file_x}")
        
        # Update x.py with NEW content
        spider_content_x_v2 = '''import scrapy

class TestSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"
    start_urls = ["http://example.com"]
    
    def parse(self, response):
        return {"source": "x_file", "version": "v2_updated"}
'''
        
        with open(spider_file_x, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content_x_v2)
        print("Updated x.py with v2 content")
        
        # Compile x.py to x.pyc as well
        x_pyc = os.path.join(pycache_dir, 'x.cpython-39.pyc')
        py_compile.compile(spider_file_x, x_pyc, doraise=True)
        print(f"Created x.pyc: {x_pyc}")
        
        # BUT keep the OLD y.pyc (this simulates the stale file issue)
        print(f"Keeping old y.pyc: {os.path.exists(y_pyc)}")
        
        # Clear sys.modules to simulate fresh start
        modules_to_remove = [k for k in sys.modules.keys() if k.startswith('test_spiders')]
        for mod in modules_to_remove:
            del sys.modules[mod]
            
        # Create NEW spider loader
        settings = Settings({'SPIDER_MODULES': ['test_spiders']})
        spider_loader_new = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Try to load the spider
        try:
            spider_cls_new = spider_loader_new.load('test_spider')
            print(f"\nLoaded spider: {spider_cls_new.__name__}")
            spider_module_new = sys.modules[spider_cls_new.__module__]
            print(f"Module: {spider_cls_new.__module__}")
            print(f"File: {spider_module_new.__file__}")
            
            # The critical test: which source file content did we actually get?
            # We'll check by instantiating the spider and calling parse
            spider_instance = spider_cls_new()
            mock_response = type('MockResponse', (), {'url': 'http://example.com'})()
            result = list(spider_instance.parse(mock_response))[0]
            
            print(f"Parsed result: {result}")
            if result.get('version') == 'v2_updated':
                print("✓ SUCCESS: Loaded updated spider content")
            elif result.get('version') == 'v1_original':
                print("🐛 ISSUE: Loaded OLD spider content from stale .pyc!")
            else:
                print("? UNKNOWN: Unexpected content")
                
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"✗ Error loading spider: {e}")
            import traceback
            traceback.print_exc()
            
        # Show all .pyc files
        print(f"\nAll .pyc files:")
        if os.path.exists(pycache_dir):
            for pyc_file in Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc'):
                print(f"  {pyc_file}")
                
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        if test_dir in sys.path:
            sys.path.remove(test_dir)
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)

"""
Simple test to demonstrate the .pyc file loading issue.
"""
import os
import sys
import tempfile
import shutil
import py_compile
from pathlib import Path

# Add the scrapy directory to the path
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/scrapy__scrapy__1.3')

from scrapy.settings import Settings
from scrapy.spiderloader import SpiderLoader


def test_simple_pyc_issue():
    """Demonstrate the core issue: stale .pyc files interfering with spider loading."""
    
    # Create a temporary directory for our test
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='simple_pyc_test_')
    spiders_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, 'test_spiders')
    os.makedirs(spiders_dir)
    
    try:
        print(f"=== Simple .pyc Issue Test ===")
        print(f"Test directory: {test_dir}")
        
        # Add the test directory to Python path
        sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
        
        # Create spider file with OLD content
        spider_content_old = '''import scrapy

class TestSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"
    custom_setting = "OLD_VERSION"
    
    def parse(self, response):
        yield {"version": "old"}
'''
        
        spider_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'spider.py')
        with open(spider_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content_old)
        print(f"Created spider.py with OLD content")
        
        # Create __init__.py
        init_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__init__.py')
        with open(init_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write('')
            
        # Load once to create .pyc file
        settings = Settings({'SPIDER_MODULES': ['test_spiders']})
        spider_loader1 = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Verify initial load
        spider_cls1 = spider_loader1.load('test_spider')
        print(f"✓ Initial load: {spider_cls1.__name__}")
        print(f"  Module: {spider_cls1.__module__}")
        print(f"  File: {sys.modules[spider_cls1.__module__].__file__}")
        
        # Check that .pyc was created
        pycache_dir = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__pycache__')
        old_pyc_files = list(Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc')) if os.path.exists(pycache_dir) else []
        print(f"Created .pyc files: {[str(f) for f in old_pyc_files]}")
        
        # NOW simulate the problematic scenario:
        # 1. User has spider.py and spider.pyc 
        # 2. User renames spider.py to new_spider.py
        # 3. User updates new_spider.py with new content
        # 4. But spider.pyc still exists
        
        # Step 1: Rename spider.py to new_spider.py
        new_spider_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'new_spider.py')
        os.rename(spider_file, new_spider_file)
        print(f"\n1. Renamed spider.py → new_spider.py")
        
        # Step 2: Update content to NEW version
        spider_content_new = '''import scrapy

class TestSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"
    custom_setting = "NEW_VERSION"  # This should be different
    
    def parse(self, response):
        yield {"version": "new"}  # This should be different
'''
        
        with open(new_spider_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content_new)
        print(f"2. Updated new_spider.py with NEW content")
        
        # Step 3: The problematic part - old .pyc still exists
        print(f"3. Old .pyc files still exist: {len(old_pyc_files) > 0}")
        
        # Step 4: Clear sys.modules and create NEW spider loader (simulating fresh scrapy run)
        modules_to_remove = [k for k in sys.modules.keys() if k.startswith('test_spiders')]
        for mod in modules_to_remove:
            del sys.modules[mod]
            
        print(f"\n4. Creating NEW spider loader (simulating 'scrapy crawl')")
        spider_loader2 = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Try to load the spider
        spider_cls2 = spider_loader2.load('test_spider')
        print(f"✓ Loaded spider: {spider_cls2.__name__}")
        print(f"  Module: {spider_cls2.__module__}")
        print(f"  File: {sys.modules[spider_cls2.__module__].__file__}")
        
        # The CRITICAL TEST: Check if we got the NEW or OLD content
        # We can check the class attributes or by running the spider
        if hasattr(spider_cls2, 'custom_setting'):
            setting_value = getattr(spider_cls2, 'custom_setting', 'UNKNOWN')
            print(f"  Custom setting: {setting_value}")
            
            if setting_value == "NEW_VERSION":
                print("  ✓ SUCCESS: Got NEW content")
                result = "SUCCESS"
            elif setting_value == "OLD_VERSION":
                print("  🐛 BUG: Got OLD content from stale .pyc!")
                result = "BUG"
            else:
                print("  ? UNKNOWN: Unexpected setting value")
                result = "UNKNOWN"
        else:
            print("  ? Could not check custom setting")
            result = "CHECK_FAILED"
            
        # Also test by running the spider
        try:
            spider_instance = spider_cls2()
            mock_response = type('MockResponse', (), {'url': 'http://example.com'})()
            results = list(spider_instance.parse(mock_response))
            if results:
                version_result = results[0].get('version', 'unknown')
                print(f"  Parse result version: {version_result}")
                if version_result == 'new':
                    print("  ✓ Parse confirms NEW content")
                elif version_result == 'old':
                    print("  🐛 Parse shows OLD content!")
                else:
                    print("  ? Unexpected parse result")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"  Error testing parse: {e}")
            
        return result
        
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        if test_dir in sys.path:
            sys.path.remove(test_dir)
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)


if __name__ == '__main__':
    result = test_simple_pyc_issue()
    print(f"\n=== RESULT ===")

    elif result == "SUCCESS":
        print("✅ No bug detected: Correct spider content loaded")

The problem is that the module discovery (`walk_modules`) is looking for Python modules based on the `.py` files in the directory. When I rename `spider.py` to `new_spider.py`, the `walk_modules` function tries to import `test_spiders.new_spider`, but that fails because there's no such module mapping.

But wait, that's not quite right either. Let me think about this differently. The real issue from the bug report is that there are TWO files: `y.py` and `y.pyc` exist together. Then the user does `mv y.py x.py` and updates `x.py`. When they run `scrapy crawl spider`, it loads the old spider from `y.pyc` instead of the new one from `x.py`.

But that doesn't make sense either, because if they rename `y.py` to `x.py`, then `y.py` no longer exists, so the spider discovery wouldn't find it anyway.

Let me re-read the issue more carefully...

Actually, let me think about this differently. What if the issue is that BOTH `y.py` and `x.py` exist (maybe the user copied instead of moved), AND both have corresponding `.pyc` files?

     """
     return CAMELCASE_INVALID_CHARS.sub('', string.title())

+
+# shutil.copytree from Python 2.6 (for backwards compatibility)
+import fnmatch
+import os
+from shutil import copy2, copystat, WindowsError
+
+
+def ignore_patterns(*patterns):
+    def _ignore_patterns(path, names):
+        ignored_names = []
+        for pattern in patterns:
+            ignored_names.extend(fnmatch.filter(names, pattern))
+        return set(ignored_names)
+    return _ignore_patterns
+
+
+def copytree(src, dst, symlinks=False, ignore=None):
+    names = os.listdir(src)
+    if ignore is not None:
+        ignored_names = ignore(src, names)
+    else:
+        ignored_names = set()
+
+    os.makedirs(dst)
+    errors = []
+    for name in names:
+        if name in ignored_names:
+            continue
+        srcname = os.path.join(src, name)
+        dstname = os.path.join(dst, name)
+        try:
+            if symlinks and os.path.islink(srcname):
+                linkto = os.readlink(srcname)
+                os.symlink(linkto, dstname)
+            elif os.path.isdir(srcname):
+                copytree(srcname, dstname, symlinks, ignore)
+            else:
+                copy2(srcname, dstname)
+            # XXX What about devices, sockets etc.?
+        except (IOError, os.error), why:
+            errors.append((srcname, dstname, str(why)))
+        # catch the Error from the recursive copytree so that we can
+        # continue with other files
+        except Error, err:
+            errors.extend(err.args[0])
+    try:
+        copystat(src, dst)
+    except OSError, why:
+        if WindowsError is not None and isinstance(why, WindowsError):
+            # Copying file access times may fail on Windows
+            pass
+        else:
+            errors.extend((src, dst, str(why)))
+    if errors:
+        raise Error, errors
+

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I understand the issue better. Let me reanalyze:

1. User has a spider named "spider" defined in file `y.py`
2. Both `y.py` and `y.pyc` exist in the spiders directory
3. User renames `y.py` to `x.py` using `mv y.py x.py`
4. User updates the content of `x.py` 
5. When running `scrapy crawl spider`, it loads the old version from `y.pyc` instead of the updated `x.py`

So the key insight is:
- The spider NAME is still "spider" (not changing)
- The FILE containing the spider is being renamed from `y.py` to `x.py`
- But the old `y.pyc` file still exists
- Python's import system loads `y.pyc` instead of properly loading `x.py`

The problem is in the spider loading mechanism. When `walk_modules` discovers spider modules, it uses `pkgutil.iter_modules` which looks at the filesystem for .py files. But when it tries to import those modules using `import_module`, Python's import system may load stale .pyc files.

Looking at the code in `scrapy/utils/misc.py`:
```python
def walk_modules(path):
    mods = []
    mod = import_module(path)
    mods.append(mod)
    if hasattr(mod, '__path__'):
        for _, subpath, ispkg in iter_modules(mod.__path__):
            fullpath = path + '.' + subpath
            if ispkg:
                mods += walk_modules(fullpath)
            else:
                submod = import_module(fullpath)  # <-- This is where the issue happens
                mods.append(submod)
    return mods
```

The issue is that `import_module(fullpath)` may load a stale .pyc file instead of the actual .py file.

However, looking at this more carefully, I think the issue might be different. Let me trace through what should happen:

1. Initially: `y.py` exists with spider named "spider", `y.pyc` gets created
2. After rename: `x.py` exists with updated spider named "spider", but `y.pyc` still exists
3. When scrapy loads spiders, it should discover `x.py` and import `test_spiders.x`
4. But if `y.pyc` is somehow getting loaded instead, that would be the bug

Actually, wait. I think the issue is that Python's module system is finding the old `y.pyc` file and associating it with the module name that contains a spider named "spider".

Let me look at this from a different angle. What if the issue is that when there are multiple files that define a spider with the same name, the spider loader picks the wrong one, and the presence of stale .pyc files affects this selection?

Or maybe the issue is simpler: when Python tries to import a module and both .py and .pyc exist, it should load the .py file if it's newer, but there's some bug in this logic.

The issue says:
1. Have `y.py` and `y.pyc` in spider directory
2. Rename with `mv y.py x.py` and update `x.py`
3. Run `scrapy crawl spider` - uses old version instead of updates in `x.py`

So the spider name is "spider", it was in `y.py`, now it's in `x.py`, but `y.pyc` still exists.

"""
Correct test to reproduce the exact .pyc file loading issue from the bug report.

Scenario:
1. Have spider named "spider" in file `y.py` with `y.pyc` existing
2. Rename `y.py` to `x.py` and update content

import os
import sys
import tempfile
import shutil
import py_compile
from pathlib import Path

# Add the scrapy directory to the path
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/scrapy__scrapy__1.3')

from scrapy.settings import Settings
from scrapy.spiderloader import SpiderLoader


def test_correct_pyc_issue():
    """Test the exact scenario from the bug report."""
    
    # Create a temporary directory for our test
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='correct_pyc_test_')
    spiders_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, 'test_spiders')
    os.makedirs(spiders_dir)
    
    try:
        print(f"=== Testing Exact Bug Report Scenario ===")
        print(f"Test directory: {test_dir}")
        
        # Add the test directory to Python path
        sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
        
        # STEP 1: Create initial spider file y.py with spider named "spider"
        spider_content_old = '''import scrapy

class MySpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "spider"  # Spider name is "spider"
    start_urls = ["http://example.com"]
    
    def parse(self, response):
        yield {"version": "old", "source": "y_file"}
'''
        
        spider_file_y = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'y.py')
        with open(spider_file_y, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content_old)
        print(f"1. Created y.py with spider named 'spider'")
        
        # Create __init__.py
        init_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__init__.py')
        with open(init_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write('')
            
        # Load once to create .pyc file (simulating previous runs)
        settings = Settings({'SPIDER_MODULES': ['test_spiders']})
        spider_loader1 = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Verify initial load
        spider_cls1 = spider_loader1.load('spider')
        print(f"✓ Initial load successful: {spider_cls1.__name__}")
        print(f"  Module: {spider_cls1.__module__}")
        print(f"  File: {sys.modules[spider_cls1.__module__].__file__}")
        
        # Check that .pyc was created
        pycache_dir = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__pycache__')
        old_pyc_files = list(Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc')) if os.path.exists(pycache_dir) else []
        print(f"  Created .pyc files: {len(old_pyc_files)} files")
        
        # STEP 2: Simulate the exact bug report scenario
        print(f"\n--- Simulating Bug Report Steps ---")
        
        # "Have y.py and y.pyc in spider directory" - we have both
        print(f"✓ Have y.py and y.pyc in spider directory")
        
        # "Rename it with mv y.py x.py" 
        spider_file_x = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'x.py')
        os.rename(spider_file_y, spider_file_x)
        print(f"✓ Renamed y.py → x.py using mv")
        
        # "Update the new x.py" with different content
        spider_content_new = '''import scrapy

class MySpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "spider"  # Same spider name
    start_urls = ["http://example.com"]
    
    def parse(self, response):
        yield {"version": "new", "source": "x_file"}  # Different content
'''
        
        with open(spider_file_x, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content_new)
        print(f"✓ Updated x.py with NEW content")
        
        # "After updates, run scrapy crawl spider" - simulate this by creating new loader
        # IMPORTANT: The y.pyc file still exists from before!
        print(f"✓ y.pyc still exists: {len([f for f in old_pyc_files if 'y.' in str(f)]) > 0}")
        
        # Clear sys.modules to simulate fresh scrapy process
        modules_to_remove = [k for k in sys.modules.keys() if k.startswith('test_spiders')]
        for mod in modules_to_remove:
            del sys.modules[mod]
            
        print(f"\n--- Running 'scrapy crawl spider' (creating new spider loader) ---")
        spider_loader2 = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # List available spiders to see what was discovered
        available_spiders = spider_loader2.list()
        print(f"Available spiders: {available_spiders}")
        
        # Try to load the spider (this is the critical test)
        try:
            spider_cls2 = spider_loader2.load('spider')
            print(f"✓ Loaded spider: {spider_cls2.__name__}")
            print(f"  Module: {spider_cls2.__module__}")
            
            spider_module_file = sys.modules[spider_cls2.__module__].__file__
            print(f"  File: {spider_module_file}")
            
            # CRITICAL TEST: Check if we got the NEW or OLD content
            spider_instance = spider_cls2()
            mock_response = type('MockResponse', (), {'url': 'http://example.com'})()
            results = list(spider_instance.parse(mock_response))
            
            if results:
                result = results[0]
                version = result.get('version', 'unknown')
                source = result.get('source', 'unknown')
                print(f"  Parse result: version={version}, source={source}")
                
                if version == 'new' and source == 'x_file':
                    print("  ✅ SUCCESS: Loaded UPDATED content from x.py")
                    result_status = "SUCCESS"
                elif version == 'old' and source == 'y_file':
                    print("  🐛 BUG: Loaded OLD content from y.pyc instead of x.py!")
                    result_status = "BUG"

                    print("  ? UNKNOWN: Unexpected content")
                    result_status = "UNKNOWN"
            else:
                print("  ? ERROR: No parse results")
                result_status = "ERROR"

        print(f"\nFinal .pyc files ({len(final_pyc_files)}):")
        for pyc_file in final_pyc_files:
            print(f"  {pyc_file}")
            
        return result_status
        
    finally:

        if test_dir in sys.path:
            sys.path.remove(test_dir)
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)

Based on the issue description, let me think about what the actual problem is and where to fix it:

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS

### 5.1 Problem Statement
When a spider file is renamed (e.g., from `y.py` to `x.py`) and the old `.pyc` file remains, Scrapy loads the old spider from the stale `.pyc` file instead of the updated spider from the new `.py` file.

### 5.2 Problem Location
The issue is in the spider loading mechanism, specifically in how Python's import system interacts with stale `.pyc` files. The problem likely occurs in:
- `scrapy/utils/misc.py` in the `walk_modules()` function
- `scrapy/spiderloader.py` in the `_load_all_spiders()` method

### 5.3 Root Cause Analysis
When `walk_modules()` discovers Python modules using `pkgutil.iter_modules()`, it finds `.py` files. However, when it imports these modules using `import_module()`, Python's import system may load stale `.pyc` files instead of recompiling from the `.py` files, especially when there are naming conflicts or timestamp issues.

### 5.4 Best Practices for Fix
- Make minimal changes to avoid breaking existing functionality
- Handle `.pyc` file cleanup or invalidation properly
- Ensure the fix works across different Python versions supported by Scrapy 1.3
- Don't break the existing spider discovery mechanism

### 5.5 Proposed Solution
The fix should ensure that when loading spider modules, Scrapy uses the most up-to-date source files rather than stale bytecode files. This can be accomplished by:

1. **Option 1**: Clean up stale `.pyc` files before loading modules
2. **Option 2**: Force Python to recompile `.py` files when they're newer than `.pyc` files  
3. **Option 3**: Modify the import mechanism to prefer source files over stale bytecode

Option 1 (cleanup) seems safest and most reliable.

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION

    """Loads a module and all its submodules from the given module path and
    returns them. If *any* module throws an exception while importing, that
    exception is thrown back.

    For example: walk_modules('scrapy.utils')
    """

    mods = []

    mods.append(mod)
    if hasattr(mod, '__path__'):
