def _cleanup_stale_pyc_files(package_path):
    """
    Clean up stale .pyc files that don't have corresponding .py files.
    This prevents Python from loading outdated bytecode when spider files are renamed.
    """
    if not os.path.exists(package_path) or not os.path.isdir(package_path):
        return
        
    # Get all .py files (without extension)  
    py_files = set()
    for filename in os.listdir(package_path):
        if filename.endswith('.py') and filename != '__init__.py':
            py_files.add(filename[:-3])  # Remove .py extension
            
    # Check __pycache__ directory if it exists
    pycache_path = os.path.join(package_path, '__pycache__')
    if os.path.exists(pycache_path) and os.path.isdir(pycache_path):
        # Find .pyc files that don't have corresponding .py files
        for filename in os.listdir(pycache_path):
            if filename.endswith('.pyc'):
                # Extract module name from .pyc filename
                # Format: module.cpython-39.pyc or module.pyc
                if '.cpython-' in filename:
                    module_name = filename.split('.cpython-')[0]
                else:
                    module_name = filename[:-4]  # Remove .pyc
                    
                # If there's no corresponding .py file, remove the stale .pyc
                if module_name not in py_files and module_name != '__init__':
                    pyc_file_path = os.path.join(pycache_path, filename)
                    try:
                        os.remove(pyc_file_path)
                    except (OSError, IOError):
                        # Ignore errors during cleanup
                        pass


def walk_modules(path):
    """Loads a module and all its submodules from the given module path and
    returns them. If *any* module throws an exception while importing, that
    exception is thrown back.

    For example: walk_modules('scrapy.utils')
    """

    mods = []
    mod = import_module(path)
    mods.append(mod)
    
    # Clean up stale .pyc files to prevent loading outdated bytecode
    if hasattr(mod, '__path__') and hasattr(mod, '__file__'):
        package_path = os.path.dirname(mod.__file__)
        _cleanup_stale_pyc_files(package_path)
        
    if hasattr(mod, '__path__'):
        for _, subpath, ispkg in iter_modules(mod.__path__):
            fullpath = path + '.' + subpath
            if ispkg:
                mods += walk_modules(fullpath)
            else:
                submod = import_module(fullpath)
                mods.append(submod)
    return mods</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>_ITERABLE_SINGLE_VALUES = dict, BaseItem, six.text_type, bytes

def _cleanup_stale_pyc_files(package_path):
    """
    Clean up stale .pyc files that don't have corresponding .py files.
    This prevents Python from loading outdated bytecode when spider files are renamed.
    """
    if not os.path.exists(package_path) or not os.path.isdir(package_path):
        return
        
    # Get all .py files (without extension)  
    py_files = set()
    for filename in os.listdir(package_path):
        if filename.endswith('.py') and filename != '__init__.py':
            py_files.add(filename[:-3])  # Remove .py extension
            
    # Check __pycache__ directory if it exists
    pycache_path = os.path.join(package_path, '__pycache__')
    if os.path.exists(pycache_path) and os.path.isdir(pycache_path):
        # Find .pyc files that don't have corresponding .py files
        for filename in os.listdir(pycache_path):
            if filename.endswith('.pyc'):
                # Extract module name from .pyc filename
                # Format: module.cpython-39.pyc or module.pyc
                if '.cpython-' in filename:
                    module_name = filename.split('.cpython-')[0]
                else:
                    module_name = filename[:-4]  # Remove .pyc
                    
                # If there's no corresponding .py file, remove the stale .pyc
                if module_name not in py_files and module_name != '__init__':
                    pyc_file_path = os.path.join(pycache_path, filename)
                    try:
                        os.remove(pyc_file_path)
                    except (OSError, IOError):
                        # Ignore errors during cleanup
                        pass

    59	    exception is thrown back.
    60	
    61	    For example: walk_modules('scrapy.utils')

    78	def extract_regex(regex, text, encoding='utf-8'):
    79	    """Extract a list of unicode strings from the given text/encoding using the following policies:
    80	
    81	    * if the regex contains a named group called "extract" that will be returned
    82	    * if the regex contains multiple numbered groups, all those will be returned (flattened)
    83	    * if the regex doesn't contain any group the entire regex matching is returned
    84	    """
    85	
    86	    if isinstance(regex, six.string_types):
    87	        regex = re.compile(regex, re.UNICODE)
    88	
    89	    try:
    90	        strings = [regex.search(text).group('extract')]   # named group
    91	    except:
    92	        strings = regex.findall(text)    # full regex or numbered groups
    93	    strings = flatten(strings)
    94	
    95	    if isinstance(text, six.text_type):
    96	        return [replace_entities(s, keep=['lt', 'amp']) for s in strings]
    97	    else:
    98	        return [replace_entities(to_unicode(s, encoding), keep=['lt', 'amp'])
    99	                for s in strings]
   100	
   101	
   102	def md5sum(file):
   103	    """Calculate the md5 checksum of a file-like object without reading its
   104	    whole content in memory.
   105	
   106	    >>> from io import BytesIO
   107	    >>> md5sum(BytesIO(b'file content to hash'))
   108	    '784406af91dd5a54fbb9c84c2236595a'
   109	    """
   110	    m = hashlib.md5()
   111	    while True:
   112	        d = file.read(8096)
   113	        if not d:
   114	            break
   115	        m.update(d)
   116	    return m.hexdigest()
   117	
   118	
   119	def rel_has_nofollow(rel):
   120	    """Return True if link rel attribute has nofollow type"""
   121	    return True if rel is not None and 'nofollow' in rel.split() else False

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now let me add the helper function in the right place:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/scrapy__scrapy__1.3/scrapy/utils/misc.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def extract_regex(regex, text, encoding='utf-8'):
    """Extract a list of unicode strings from the given text/encoding using the following policies:

    * if the regex contains a named group called "extract" that will be returned
    * if the regex contains multiple numbered groups, all those will be returned (flattened)
    * if the regex doesn't contain any group the entire regex matching is returned
    """

    if isinstance(regex, six.string_types):
        regex = re.compile(regex, re.UNICODE)

    try:
        strings = [regex.search(text).group('extract')]   # named group
    except:
        strings = regex.findall(text)    # full regex or numbered groups
    strings = flatten(strings)

    if isinstance(text, six.text_type):
        return [replace_entities(s, keep=['lt', 'amp']) for s in strings]
    else:
        return [replace_entities(to_unicode(s, encoding), keep=['lt', 'amp'])
                for s in strings]


def _cleanup_stale_pyc_files(package_path):
    """
    Clean up stale .pyc files that don't have corresponding .py files.
    This prevents Python from loading outdated bytecode when spider files are renamed.
    """
    if not os.path.exists(package_path) or not os.path.isdir(package_path):
        return
        
    # Get all .py files (without extension)  
    py_files = set()
    for filename in os.listdir(package_path):
        if filename.endswith('.py') and filename != '__init__.py':
            py_files.add(filename[:-3])  # Remove .py extension
            
    # Check __pycache__ directory if it exists
    pycache_path = os.path.join(package_path, '__pycache__')
    if os.path.exists(pycache_path) and os.path.isdir(pycache_path):
        # Find .pyc files that don't have corresponding .py files
        for filename in os.listdir(pycache_path):
            if filename.endswith('.pyc'):
                # Extract module name from .pyc filename
                # Format: module.cpython-39.pyc or module.pyc
                if '.cpython-' in filename:
                    module_name = filename.split('.cpython-')[0]
                else:
                    module_name = filename[:-4]  # Remove .pyc
                    
                # If there's no corresponding .py file, remove the stale .pyc
                if module_name not in py_files and module_name != '__init__':
                    pyc_file_path = os.path.join(pycache_path, filename)
                    try:
                        os.remove(pyc_file_path)
                    except (OSError, IOError):
                        # Ignore errors during cleanup
                        pass


def md5sum(file):</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def extract_regex(regex, text, encoding='utf-8'):
    """Extract a list of unicode strings from the given text/encoding using the following policies:

    * if the regex contains a named group called "extract" that will be returned
    * if the regex contains multiple numbered groups, all those will be returned (flattened)
    * if the regex doesn't contain any group the entire regex matching is returned
    """

    if isinstance(regex, six.string_types):
        regex = re.compile(regex, re.UNICODE)

    try:
        strings = [regex.search(text).group('extract')]   # named group
    except:
        strings = regex.findall(text)    # full regex or numbered groups
    strings = flatten(strings)

    if isinstance(text, six.text_type):
        return [replace_entities(s, keep=['lt', 'amp']) for s in strings]
    else:
        return [replace_entities(to_unicode(s, encoding), keep=['lt', 'amp'])
                for s in strings]

    """Loads a module and all its submodules from the given module path and
    returns them. If *any* module throws an exception while importing, that
    exception is thrown back.

    For example: walk_modules('scrapy.utils')
    """

    mods = []
    mod = import_module(path)
    mods.append(mod)
    
    # Clean up stale .pyc files to prevent loading outdated bytecode
    if hasattr(mod, '__path__') and hasattr(mod, '__file__'):

        _cleanup_stale_pyc_files(package_path)

"""
Test to verify that the .pyc cleanup fix works correctly.
"""
import os
import sys
import tempfile
import shutil
import py_compile
from pathlib import Path

# Add the scrapy directory to the path
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/scrapy__scrapy__1.3')

from scrapy.settings import Settings
from scrapy.spiderloader import SpiderLoader
from scrapy.utils.misc import _cleanup_stale_pyc_files


def test_pyc_cleanup_function():
    """Test the _cleanup_stale_pyc_files function directly."""
    
    # Create a temporary directory for our test
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='pyc_cleanup_test_')
    
    try:
        print(f"=== Testing _cleanup_stale_pyc_files function ===")
        print(f"Test directory: {test_dir}")
        
        # Create some .py files
        py_files = ['spider1.py', 'spider2.py', '__init__.py']
        for filename in py_files:
            filepath = os.path.join(test_dir, filename)
            with open(filepath, 'w') as f:
                f.write('# Test file\n')
                
        # Create __pycache__ directory
        pycache_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, '__pycache__')
        os.makedirs(pycache_dir)
        
        # Create some .pyc files - some should be cleaned up, some should remain
        # These should REMAIN (have corresponding .py files)
        valid_pyc_files = [
            'spider1.cpython-39.pyc',
            'spider2.cpython-39.pyc', 
            '__init__.cpython-39.pyc'
        ]
        
        # These should be CLEANED UP (no corresponding .py files)
        stale_pyc_files = [
            'old_spider.cpython-39.pyc',
            'renamed_spider.cpython-39.pyc'
        ]
        
        all_pyc_files = valid_pyc_files + stale_pyc_files
        for filename in all_pyc_files:
            filepath = os.path.join(pycache_dir, filename)
            with open(filepath, 'w') as f:
                f.write('# Fake .pyc content\n')
                
        print(f"Created {len(py_files)} .py files")
        print(f"Created {len(all_pyc_files)} .pyc files ({len(stale_pyc_files)} stale)")
        
        # Verify initial state
        initial_pyc_count = len(list(Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc')))
        print(f"Initial .pyc files: {initial_pyc_count}")
        
        # Run cleanup
        _cleanup_stale_pyc_files(test_dir)
        
        # Check results
        remaining_pyc_files = list(Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc'))
        remaining_filenames = [f.name for f in remaining_pyc_files]
        
        print(f"After cleanup: {len(remaining_pyc_files)} .pyc files remain")
        print(f"Remaining files: {sorted(remaining_filenames)}")
        
        # Validate results
        expected_remaining = set(valid_pyc_files)
        actually_remaining = set(remaining_filenames)
        
        if expected_remaining == actually_remaining:
            print("✅ SUCCESS: Cleanup worked correctly")
            print("  - Valid .pyc files preserved")
            print("  - Stale .pyc files removed")
            return True
        else:
            print("❌ FAILURE: Cleanup didn't work as expected")
            print(f"  Expected remaining: {sorted(expected_remaining)}")
            print(f"  Actually remaining: {sorted(actually_remaining)}")
            return False
            
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"❌ ERROR: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False
        
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)


def test_integration_scenario():
    """Test the integration scenario that mimics the bug report."""
    
    # Create a temporary directory for our test
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='integration_test_')
    spiders_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, 'test_spiders')
    os.makedirs(spiders_dir)
    
    try:
        print(f"\n=== Testing Integration Scenario ===")
        print(f"Test directory: {test_dir}")
        
        # Add the test directory to Python path
        sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
        
        # STEP 1: Create initial spider file y.py
        spider_content_old = '''import scrapy

class MySpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"
    custom_setting = "OLD_VERSION"
    
    def parse(self, response):
        yield {"version": "old", "source": "y_file"}
'''
        
        spider_file_y = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'y.py')
        with open(spider_file_y, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content_old)
        print(f"1. Created y.py with spider")
        
        # Create __init__.py
        init_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__init__.py')
        with open(init_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write('')
            
        # Load once to create .pyc files
        settings = Settings({'SPIDER_MODULES': ['test_spiders']})
        spider_loader1 = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Verify initial load
        spider_cls1 = spider_loader1.load('test_spider')
        old_setting = getattr(spider_cls1, 'custom_setting', 'MISSING')
        print(f"✓ Initial load: custom_setting={old_setting}")
        
        # Check .pyc files were created
        pycache_dir = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__pycache__')
        old_pyc_files = list(Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc')) if os.path.exists(pycache_dir) else []
        print(f"  Created .pyc files: {len(old_pyc_files)}")
        
        # Manually create a stale .pyc file to simulate the bug condition
        # This simulates the scenario where y.pyc exists from previous runs
        if old_pyc_files:
            y_pyc_path = os.path.join(pycache_dir, 'y.cpython-39.pyc')
            # Just touch the file to make sure it exists
            with open(y_pyc_path, 'w') as f:
                f.write('# Stale .pyc file\n')
            print(f"  Ensured y.pyc exists (stale)")
        
        # STEP 2: Simulate the bug report scenario
        # "rename it with mv y.py x.py and update the new x.py"
        spider_file_x = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'x.py')
        os.rename(spider_file_y, spider_file_x)
        print(f"2. Renamed y.py → x.py (simulating mv command)")
        
        # "update the new x.py" with different content
        spider_content_new = '''import scrapy

class MySpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"  # Same spider name
    custom_setting = "NEW_VERSION"  # Updated setting
    
    def parse(self, response):
        yield {"version": "new", "source": "x_file"}
'''
        
        with open(spider_file_x, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content_new)
        print(f"3. Updated x.py with NEW content")
        
        # Verify that y.pyc still exists (this is the bug condition)
        y_pyc_exists = os.path.exists(os.path.join(pycache_dir, 'y.cpython-39.pyc'))
        print(f"4. y.pyc still exists: {y_pyc_exists}")
        
        # STEP 3: "run scrapy crawl spider" - simulate by creating new spider loader
        # Clear sys.modules to simulate fresh scrapy process
        modules_to_remove = [k for k in sys.modules.keys() if k.startswith('test_spiders')]
        for mod in modules_to_remove:
            del sys.modules[mod]
            
        print(f"5. Creating new spider loader (simulating scrapy crawl)")
        
        # This should trigger our fix in walk_modules
        spider_loader2 = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Check what happened to the .pyc files
        remaining_pyc_files = list(Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc')) if os.path.exists(pycache_dir) else []
        remaining_names = [f.name for f in remaining_pyc_files]
        print(f"6. Remaining .pyc files: {remaining_names}")
        
        # y.pyc should have been cleaned up by our fix
        y_pyc_still_exists = any('y.cpython' in name for name in remaining_names)
        print(f"   y.pyc cleaned up: {not y_pyc_still_exists}")
        
        # Try to load the spider
        spider_cls2 = spider_loader2.load('test_spider')
        new_setting = getattr(spider_cls2, 'custom_setting', 'MISSING')
        print(f"7. Loaded spider: custom_setting={new_setting}")
        
        # Test by running the spider
        spider_instance = spider_cls2()
        mock_response = type('MockResponse', (), {'url': 'http://example.com'})()
        results = list(spider_instance.parse(mock_response))
        if results:
            result = results[0]
            version = result.get('version', 'unknown')
            source = result.get('source', 'unknown')
            print(f"   Parse result: version={version}, source={source}")
        
        # Determine success
        if new_setting == "NEW_VERSION" and not y_pyc_still_exists:
            print("✅ SUCCESS: Fix worked!")
            print("   - Loaded updated spider content")
            print("   - Cleaned up stale .pyc file")
            return True
        elif new_setting == "OLD_VERSION":
            print("❌ FAILURE: Still loading old spider content")
            return False
        else:
            print(f"? UNCLEAR: custom_setting={new_setting}, y_pyc_still_exists={y_pyc_still_exists}")
            return False
        
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"❌ ERROR: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False
        
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        if test_dir in sys.path:
            sys.path.remove(test_dir)
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)

This could be integrated into the main test suite.

import os
import tempfile

from scrapy.utils.misc import _cleanup_stale_pyc_files


def test_cleanup_stale_pyc_files():
    """Test the _cleanup_stale_pyc_files function."""
    
    # Create a temporary directory for our test
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='unit_test_pyc_cleanup_')
    
    try:
        # Create some .py files
        py_files = ['module1.py', 'module2.py', '__init__.py']
        for filename in py_files:
            filepath = os.path.join(test_dir, filename)
            with open(filepath, 'w') as f:
                f.write('# Test Python file\n')
                
        # Create __pycache__ directory
        pycache_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, '__pycache__')
        os.makedirs(pycache_dir)
        
        # Create .pyc files - mix of valid and stale ones
        valid_pyc_files = [
            'module1.cpython-39.pyc',
            'module2.cpython-39.pyc',
            '__init__.cpython-39.pyc'
        ]
        
        stale_pyc_files = [
            'old_module.cpython-39.pyc',
            'deleted_module.cpython-39.pyc',
            'renamed_module.pyc'  # Different format
        ]
        
        all_pyc_files = valid_pyc_files + stale_pyc_files
        for filename in all_pyc_files:
            filepath = os.path.join(pycache_dir, filename)
            with open(filepath, 'w') as f:
                f.write('# Fake .pyc content\n')
        
        # Verify initial state
        initial_count = len(list(Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc')))
        assert initial_count == len(all_pyc_files), f"Expected {len(all_pyc_files)} initial files, got {initial_count}"
        
        # Run cleanup
        _cleanup_stale_pyc_files(test_dir)
        
        # Check results
        remaining_files = list(Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc'))
        remaining_names = set(f.name for f in remaining_files)
        
        expected_remaining = set(valid_pyc_files)
        
        # Validate
        assert remaining_names == expected_remaining, f"Expected {expected_remaining}, got {remaining_names}"
        
        print("✅ Unit test passed!")
        return True
        
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"❌ Unit test failed: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False
        
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)


def test_cleanup_edge_cases():
    """Test edge cases for _cleanup_stale_pyc_files."""
    
    # Test with non-existent directory
    try:
        _cleanup_stale_pyc_files('/non/existent/directory')
        print("✅ Edge case 1 passed: Non-existent directory handled")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"❌ Edge case 1 failed: {e}")
        return False
    
    # Test with file instead of directory
    test_file = tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(delete=False)
    try:
        test_file.close()
        _cleanup_stale_pyc_files(test_file.name)
        print("✅ Edge case 2 passed: File path handled")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"❌ Edge case 2 failed: {e}")
        return False
    finally:
        os.unlink(test_file.name)
    
    # Test with directory without __pycache__
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='edge_case_test_')
    try:
        # Create some .py files
        with open(os.path.join(test_dir, 'test.py'), 'w') as f:
            f.write('# Test file\n')
        
        # No __pycache__ directory
        _cleanup_stale_pyc_files(test_dir)
        print("✅ Edge case 3 passed: No __pycache__ directory handled")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"❌ Edge case 3 failed: {e}")
        return False
    finally:
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)
        
    return True

"""
Comprehensive test to make sure the fix handles various scenarios correctly.
"""
import os
import sys
import tempfile
import shutil
from pathlib import Path

# Add the scrapy directory to the path
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/scrapy__scrapy__1.3')

from scrapy.settings import Settings
from scrapy.spiderloader import SpiderLoader


def test_multiple_renames():
    """Test multiple renames to make sure cleanup works repeatedly."""
    
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='multi_rename_test_')
    spiders_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, 'test_spiders')
    os.makedirs(spiders_dir)
    
    try:
        print("=== Testing Multiple Renames ===")
        sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
        
        # Create initial spider
        spider_content = '''import scrapy

class TestSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"
    version = "{version}"
    
    def parse(self, response):
        yield {{"version": "{version}"}}
'''
        
        # Sequence of renames: file1.py -> file2.py -> file3.py
        filenames = ['file1.py', 'file2.py', 'file3.py']
        
        # Create first file
        current_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, filenames[0])
        with open(current_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content.format(version='v1'))
            
        # Create __init__.py
        with open(os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__init__.py'), 'w') as f:
            f.write('')
        
        # Do the sequence of renames
        for i in range(len(filenames) - 1):
            old_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, filenames[i])
            new_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, filenames[i + 1])
            
            # Load spider to create .pyc
            settings = Settings({'SPIDER_MODULES': ['test_spiders']})
            spider_loader = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
            spider_cls = spider_loader.load('test_spider')
            current_version = getattr(spider_cls, 'version', 'unknown')
            print(f"  Loaded spider from {filenames[i]}: version={current_version}")
            
            # Rename file
            os.rename(old_file, new_file)
            
            # Update content
            with open(new_file, 'w') as f:
                f.write(spider_content.format(version=f'v{i+2}'))
            print(f"  Renamed {filenames[i]} → {filenames[i+1]} and updated content")
        
        # Final load test
        modules_to_remove = [k for k in sys.modules.keys() if k.startswith('test_spiders')]
        for mod in modules_to_remove:
            del sys.modules[mod]
            
        settings = Settings({'SPIDER_MODULES': ['test_spiders']})
        spider_loader = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        spider_cls = spider_loader.load('test_spider')
        final_version = getattr(spider_cls, 'version', 'unknown')
        
        print(f"  Final loaded version: {final_version}")
        
        # Check .pyc files
        pycache_dir = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__pycache__')
        if os.path.exists(pycache_dir):
            pyc_files = list(Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc'))
            pyc_names = [f.name for f in pyc_files]
            print(f"  Final .pyc files: {pyc_names}")
            
            # Should only have file3.pyc and __init__.pyc
            expected_pyc = {'file3.cpython-39.pyc', '__init__.cpython-39.pyc'}
            actual_pyc = set(pyc_names)
            
            if actual_pyc == expected_pyc:
                print("  ✅ SUCCESS: Only current .pyc files remain")
                return final_version == 'v3'
            else:
                print(f"  ❌ FAILURE: Expected {expected_pyc}, got {actual_pyc}")
                return False
        else:
            print("  ❌ FAILURE: No __pycache__ directory")
            return False
        
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"  ❌ ERROR: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False
        
    finally:
        if test_dir in sys.path:
            sys.path.remove(test_dir)
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)


def test_no_pycache_directory():
    """Test that the fix works when there's no __pycache__ directory."""
    
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='no_pycache_test_')
    spiders_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, 'test_spiders')
    os.makedirs(spiders_dir)
    
    try:
        print("\n=== Testing No __pycache__ Directory ===")
        sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
        
        # Remove __pycache__ if it was automatically created
        pycache_dir = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__pycache__')
        if os.path.exists(pycache_dir):
            shutil.rmtree(pycache_dir)
        
        # Create spider file
        spider_content = '''import scrapy

class TestSpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "test_spider"
    
    def parse(self, response):
        yield {"test": "ok"}
'''
        
        spider_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'spider.py')
        with open(spider_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content)
            
        with open(os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__init__.py'), 'w') as f:
            f.write('')
        
        # This should work even without __pycache__
        settings = Settings({'SPIDER_MODULES': ['test_spiders']})
        spider_loader = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        spider_cls = spider_loader.load('test_spider')
        
        print("  ✅ SUCCESS: Worked without __pycache__ directory")
        return True
        
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"  ❌ ERROR: {e}")
        return False
        
    finally:
        if test_dir in sys.path:
            sys.path.remove(test_dir)
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)

"""
Final validation test that exactly replicates the bug report scenario.
"""
import os
import sys
import tempfile
import shutil
from pathlib import Path

# Add the scrapy directory to the path
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/scrapy__scrapy__1.3')

from scrapy.settings import Settings
from scrapy.spiderloader import SpiderLoader
from scrapy.utils.misc import _cleanup_stale_pyc_files


def test_exact_bug_report_scenario():
    """
    Test the EXACT scenario from the bug report:
    
    1. Have `y.py` and `y.pyc` in the spider directory
    2. Rename it with `mv y.py x.py` and update the new `x.py`
    3. After updates, run `scrapy crawl spider` 
    4. Should use updates in `x.py` (not old version from `y.pyc`)
    """
    
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='exact_bug_test_')
    spiders_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, 'test_spiders')
    os.makedirs(spiders_dir)
    
    try:
        print("=== Testing EXACT Bug Report Scenario ===")
        print("Replicating: Have y.py and y.pyc, mv y.py x.py, update x.py, scrapy crawl spider")
        
        sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
        
        # STEP 1: Create y.py with spider named "spider"
        spider_content_y = '''import scrapy

class MySpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "spider"  # This is the spider name used in "scrapy crawl spider"
    source_file = "y.py"
    content_version = "original"
    
    def parse(self, response):
        yield {"source": "y.py", "version": "original"}
'''
        
        y_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'y.py')
        with open(y_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content_y)
        print("1. Created y.py with spider named 'spider'")
        
        # Create __init__.py
        with open(os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__init__.py'), 'w') as f:
            f.write('')
        
        # Load once to create y.pyc (simulating previous scrapy runs)
        settings = Settings({'SPIDER_MODULES': ['test_spiders']})
        spider_loader1 = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        spider_cls1 = spider_loader1.load('spider')
        print(f"2. Initial load: source_file={getattr(spider_cls1, 'source_file', 'unknown')}")
        
        # Verify y.pyc was created
        pycache_dir = os.path.join(spiders_dir, '__pycache__')
        y_pyc_exists = (os.path.exists(pycache_dir) and 
                       any('y.cpython' in f.name for f in Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc')))
        print(f"3. Verified y.pyc exists: {y_pyc_exists}")
        
        # STEP 2: Simulate the exact commands from bug report
        # "Have y.py and y.pyc in spider directory" - ✓
        # "Then rename it with mv y.py x.py" - simulate with os.rename
        x_file = os.path.join(spiders_dir, 'x.py')
        os.rename(y_file, x_file)
        print("4. Renamed y.py → x.py (simulating 'mv y.py x.py')")
        
        # "and update the new x.py" - update with new content
        spider_content_x = '''import scrapy

class MySpider(scrapy.Spider):
    name = "spider"  # Same spider name
    source_file = "x.py"  # Updated attribute
    content_version = "updated"  # Updated attribute
    
    def parse(self, response):
        yield {"source": "x.py", "version": "updated"}  # Updated content
'''
        
        with open(x_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write(spider_content_x)
        print("5. Updated x.py with new content")
        
        # "After updates, run scrapy crawl spider" - simulate by creating new loader
        # IMPORTANT: Clear sys.modules to simulate fresh scrapy process
        modules_to_remove = [k for k in sys.modules.keys() if k.startswith('test_spiders')]
        for mod in modules_to_remove:
            del sys.modules[mod]
            
        print("6. Simulating 'scrapy crawl spider' command...")
        
        # THE KEY TEST: This should trigger our fix in walk_modules
        spider_loader2 = SpiderLoader.from_settings(settings)
        
        # Check that our cleanup function worked
        remaining_pyc_files = list(Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc')) if os.path.exists(pycache_dir) else []
        remaining_names = [f.name for f in remaining_pyc_files]
        y_pyc_still_exists = any('y.cpython' in name for name in remaining_names)
        
        print(f"7. After walk_modules cleanup:")
        print(f"   Remaining .pyc files: {remaining_names}")
        print(f"   y.pyc cleaned up: {not y_pyc_still_exists}")
        
        # Load the spider - this is the critical moment
        spider_cls2 = spider_loader2.load('spider')
        source_file = getattr(spider_cls2, 'source_file', 'unknown')
        content_version = getattr(spider_cls2, 'content_version', 'unknown')
        
        print(f"8. Loaded spider: source_file={source_file}, content_version={content_version}")
        
        # Test by actually running the spider
        spider_instance = spider_cls2()
        mock_response = type('MockResponse', (), {'url': 'http://example.com'})()
        results = list(spider_instance.parse(mock_response))
        
        if results:
            result = results[0]
            result_source = result.get('source', 'unknown')
            result_version = result.get('version', 'unknown')
            print(f"9. Parse result: source={result_source}, version={result_version}")
        else:
            result_source = 'none'
            result_version = 'none'
            print("9. No parse results")
        
        # THE VERDICT
        # SUCCESS: Should load from x.py (updated content)
        # BUG: Would load from y.pyc (old content)
        
        if (source_file == "x.py" and 
            content_version == "updated" and 
            result_source == "x.py" and 
            result_version == "updated" and
            not y_pyc_still_exists):
            print("\n🎉 SUCCESS! Bug is FIXED!")
            print("   - Loaded updated content from x.py")
            print("   - Cleaned up stale y.pyc file")
            print("   - Spider behaves as expected after rename")
            return True
        elif (source_file == "y.py" or content_version == "original"):
            print("\n🐛 BUG STILL EXISTS!")
            print("   - Loaded old content from y.pyc instead of x.py")
            print("   - Fix is not working")
            return False
        else:
            print(f"\n? UNCLEAR RESULT: source_file={source_file}, content_version={content_version}")
            print(f"   y_pyc_still_exists={y_pyc_still_exists}")
            return False
        
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"\n❌ ERROR: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False
        
    finally:
        if test_dir in sys.path:
            sys.path.remove(test_dir)
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)


def test_manual_cleanup_function():
    """Test that our cleanup function works standalone."""
    
    test_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='manual_test_')
    
    try:
        print("\n=== Testing Manual Cleanup Function ===")
        
        # Create test files
        with open(os.path.join(test_dir, 'alive.py'), 'w') as f:
            f.write('# This should remain\n')
        with open(os.path.join(test_dir, 'dead.py'), 'w') as f:
            f.write('# This will be removed\n')
            
        pycache_dir = os.path.join(test_dir, '__pycache__')
        os.makedirs(pycache_dir)
        
        # Create .pyc files
        with open(os.path.join(pycache_dir, 'alive.cpython-39.pyc'), 'w') as f:
            f.write('# Should remain\n')
        with open(os.path.join(pycache_dir, 'dead.cpython-39.pyc'), 'w') as f:
            f.write('# Should be removed\n')
        with open(os.path.join(pycache_dir, '__init__.cpython-39.pyc'), 'w') as f:
            f.write('# Should remain\n')
            
        print("1. Created test files and .pyc files")
        
        # Remove dead.py to simulate the rename scenario
        os.remove(os.path.join(test_dir, 'dead.py'))
        print("2. Removed dead.py (simulating file rename/delete)")
        
        # Run our cleanup function
        _cleanup_stale_pyc_files(test_dir)
        print("3. Ran _cleanup_stale_pyc_files()")
        
        # Check results
        remaining_pyc = list(Path(pycache_dir).glob('*.pyc'))
        remaining_names = set(f.name for f in remaining_pyc)
        expected_names = {'alive.cpython-39.pyc', '__init__.cpython-39.pyc'}
        
        print(f"4. Remaining .pyc files: {sorted(remaining_names)}")
        
        if remaining_names == expected_names:
            print("✅ SUCCESS: Manual cleanup works correctly!")
            return True
        else:
            print("❌ FAILURE: Manual cleanup didn't work as expected")
            return False
            
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"❌ ERROR: {e}")
        return False
        
    finally:
        shutil.rmtree(test_dir, ignore_errors=True)
